If so, what is the process? Who can impeach him? Has a French President been impeached?", "chosen": "In France, the President cannot be impeached in the same way as in some other countries, such as the United States. However, there is a process called \"destitution\" which is the equivalent of impeachment. This process is outlined in the French Constitution.\n\nThe destitution process can be initiated by Parliament (both the National Assembly and the Senate) if the President has committed \"a breach of his duties that is incompatible with his continuing in office.\" This process requires a two-thirds majority vote in both houses of Parliament.\n\nThe process begins with a proposal for a resolution to initiate the destitution process. This proposal must be signed by at least one-tenth of the members of the National Assembly or the Senate. If the proposal is adopted by a simple majority in both houses, a High Court is formed, composed of members of the National Assembly and the Senate.\n\nThe High Court then investigates the charges against the President and decides whether to remove him from office. If the High Court decides to remove the President, it must do so with a two-thirds majority vote.\n\nNo French President has ever been removed from office through this process.
